30	## 2. Topology and trust boundaries
31	
32	```
33	 browser  (apps/web on Modal Server)  ── anonymous id in localStorage + cookie mirror
34	    │  HTTPS JSON + SSE (reconnecting, Last-Event-ID); every request: X-Faultline-User: u_<uuid>
35	    ▼
36	 faultline-harness
37	    ├─ api          FastAPI @modal.asgi_app. No provider secret. Validates the user id, owns the HTTP
38	    │               contract (§5), spawns run_episode, and reads/writes state ONLY through Store.
39	    ├─ Store        @app.cls(max_containers=1, min_containers=1, volumes={"/data": faultline-db}).
40	    │               The single SQLite writer (§4). No secret, no fault plan, no network beyond Modal.
41	    └─ run_episode  @app.function(secrets=[anthropic-secret]). The ONLY code that sees ANTHROPIC_API_KEY.
42	                    Model loop over MCP tools; appends events to Store; evaluates; always deletes the episode.
43	    │  HTTPS: gym REST (reset / observe / evaluate / delete) + MCP streamable HTTP at /mcp (step)
44	    ▼
45	 faultline-sandbox-env
46	    └─ api          FastAPI + FastMCP. Stateless containers; episode state {sandbox_id, fault_plan,
47	                    ledger, baseline} in modal.Dict "faultline-episodes". Every tool call: load episode →
48	                    consult fault plan → (maybe) exec in the Sandbox → append to the ledger.
49	    │  modal.Sandbox.from_id(sid).exec(...)   block_network=True, no secrets, one per episode
50	    ▼
51	 Modal Sandbox      fixture repo at /workspace, python3 + pytest. Sees nothing else.
52	```
53	
54	What each process can see:
55	
56	| Process | Provider key | Fault plan / grader | Workspace files | Persistent DB | Network |
57	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
58	| browser | ✗ | ✗ (events about faults, after the fact) | via observe/events | via api only | harness only |
59	| harness `api` | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ | via Store RPC | Store + spawn |
60	| harness `Store` | ✗ | ✗ | ✗ (only what events carry) | ✓ (sole writer) | Volume only |
61	| harness `run_episode` | ✓ | ✗ | via MCP only | via Store RPC | Anthropic + sandbox-env |
62	| sandbox-env `api` | ✗ | ✓ | via Sandbox API | ✗ | Modal Sandbox only |
63	| Modal Sandbox (agent's shell) | ✗ | ✗ | ✓ | ✗ | none |
64	
65	Why three apps: the harness must be replaceable (different model / prompt) without touching the
66	environment; the environment must be gradeable without trusting the agent; the UI is static. The secret
67	boundary is enforced by Modal's per-function secrets: `api` asserts at startup that `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`
68	is *absent* from its environment and reports `has_provider_key: false` on `/health`.
69	
70	## 3. Life of a run
71	
72	1. **Identity.** On first load the SPA mints `u_<uuid4>`, stores it in `localStorage["faultline.user_id"]`
73	   and mirrors it to the `faultline_uid` cookie. Every API call carries `X-Faultline-User`. `GET /me`
74	   upserts the `users` row.
75	2. **Conversation.** The user picks a scenario in the composer. `POST /conversations {scenario_id}` creates
76	   a `conversations` row titled after the scenario. A conversation is a thread of runs; the user's
77	   "message" is the task prompt (default = scenario prompt, editable before Run).
78	3. **Run.** `POST /conversations/{id}/runs {model?, seed?, max_steps?, task_prompt?}` → Store creates a
79	   `runs` row (`queued`) → `api` calls `run_episode.spawn(run_id, …)` and returns `{run_id}` immediately
80	   (Modal web requests are capped at 150 s, so nothing long-running happens inside a request).
81	4. **Episode.** `run_episode` calls `POST /episodes` on sandbox-env (reset: create Sandbox, upload
82	   fixture, apply sticky faults, snapshot baseline), emits `run.started` and `episode.reset`, then loops:
83	   `messages.create` → for each `tool_use` call the MCP tool with `X-Faultline-Episode` → emit
84	   `tool.call` / `tool.result` (+ `fault.fired` from the ledger delta, `workspace.diff` from observe after
85	   mutating calls) → repeat until `submit`, `end_turn`, `max_steps` or an unrecoverable API error.
86	   Every `messages.create` also emits `llm.call` (usage, stop reason, request id, attempt).
87	5. **Persistence.** Events are appended to Store in per-step batches
88	   (`Store().append_events.remote(run_id, events)`), which inserts `events` rows and, in the same
89	   transaction, projects them into `messages` / `blocks` / `llm_calls` (§4.5). The loop keeps the events in
90	   memory too and re-sends the full list at `run.finished` (idempotent), so a Store restart self-heals.
91	6. **Streaming.** The browser tails `GET /runs/{id}/events` (SSE). `api` polls `Store.events_after(run_id,
92	   after_seq)` every 400 ms, closes the stream at ~110 s, and the browser reconnects with
93	   `Last-Event-ID` (= `events.seq`). A page refresh restores from `GET /runs/{id}` (whole record) or
94	   `GET /conversations/{id}` (persisted transcript) and resumes the tail.
95	7. **Grading.** `POST /episodes/{id}/evaluate` uploads hidden tests, runs pytest, removes them, and scores
96	   recovery checks from the ledger (`score = 60·tests_pass + 40·weighted checks`). `episode.evaluated`
97	   and `run.finished` update `runs.score / evaluation_json / status / tokens`; `DELETE /episodes/{id}`
98	   runs in `finally`.
99	8. **Checkpoint.** Store snapshots the DB to the Volume after `run.finished` (§4.3).
100	
101	## 4. Persistence layer: SQLite on a Modal Volume
102	
103	### 4.1 Requirements
104	
105	Persist, per anonymous browser user: conversations, runs, the full event log of each run, and the LLM
106	turns including tool calls and their results, in a shape that can (a) render a chat transcript without
107	replaying events, and (b) rebuild the exact `messages=[…]` array the model saw at each step. Survive page
108	reloads, browser restarts, and redeploys. No external database service.
109	
110	### 4.2 Modal facts that constrain the design
111	
112	- Volumes are "write-once, read-many". Writes are visible to other containers only after a commit
113	  (Modal background-commits every few seconds and on container shutdown; `.commit()` forces one).
114	  Other containers must `.reload()` to see committed changes, and reload fails while files are open.
115	- "Last write wins in case of concurrent modification of the same file" — there is no distributed file
116	  locking. Concurrent writers to one SQLite file would corrupt it.
117	- The mount is a FUSE filesystem. SQLite's WAL mode relies on a shared-memory-mapped `-shm` file and
118	  POSIX advisory locks; their behaviour on the mount is undocumented. A background commit is not a
119	  point-in-time snapshot across files, so a live DB + its journal could be captured inconsistently.
120	- Modal's own SQLite example (the Datasette cron) builds the database on local disk, copies it onto the
121	  Volume, and commits. That is the pattern to copy.
122	- Volume v2 must be requested explicitly (`version=2`); the 1.5.5 CLI still marks it experimental. v1 is
123	  fine for one file.
124	
125	### 4.3 Design: one writer, hot copy on local disk, consistent snapshots on the Volume
126	
127	```
128	 api ──.remote()──▶ ┌───────────────── Store (1 container) ─────────────────┐
129	 run_episode ──────▶│ /tmp/faultline.sqlite3   WAL, synchronous=NORMAL, FK on │
130	                    │   ▲ restore on @modal.enter        checkpoint ▼         │
131	                    │ /data/faultline.sqlite3  ◀── VACUUM INTO .tmp + os.replace + vol.commit()
132	                    └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
133	                                   Modal Volume "faultline-db" (v1)
134	```
135	
136	- **Single writer.** `Store` is a Modal class with `max_containers=1`. It is the only process that ever
137	  opens the database, so SQLite's locking assumptions hold trivially and nobody needs `reload()` — the
138	  reader *is* the writer. `min_containers=1` keeps it warm (an SSE poll must not pay a cold start plus a
139	  restore). `@modal.concurrent(max_inputs=32)` lets many `api` polls and one run's appends interleave; a
140	  `threading.Lock` serialises access to the single `sqlite3` connection (ops are sub-millisecond).
141	- **Hot copy on local disk.** The live file is `/tmp/faultline.sqlite3` on the container's own disk with
142	  ordinary SQLite settings (`journal_mode=WAL`, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `foreign_keys=ON`,
143	  `busy_timeout=5000`). No FUSE questions.
144	- **Checkpoint.** `VACUUM INTO '/data/faultline.sqlite3.tmp'` writes a complete, consistent, single-file
145	  copy; `os.replace()` renames it over `/data/faultline.sqlite3`; `vol.commit()` publishes it. Because
146	  only complete files are ever renamed into place, a background commit can never capture a half-written
147	  database. Triggers: after every `run.finished`, every 15 s while there are unsaved writes, and in
148	  `@modal.exit` (as implemented, `run.finished` currently snapshots twice: once in `append_events` and
149	  again in `finish_run`). A `checkpoint_now` local entrypoint forces one for evidence/export.
150	- **Restore.** `@modal.enter` copies `/data/faultline.sqlite3` → `/tmp` if present (else creates an empty
151	  DB), then applies migrations: `PRAGMA user_version` gates `harness/migrations/000N_*.sql`, applied in
152	  order (intended: one transaction each; today `executescript` runs, then a separate `PRAGMA
153	  user_version`, so a crash mid-migration is not atomic — fix pending with the Store owner).
154	- **Durability model.** Completed runs are durable as soon as their post-`run.finished` checkpoint commits
155	  (seconds). If the Store container dies mid-run, at most the last ≤15 s of that run's events are lost
156	  from the Volume — and they are not lost from the system: `append_events` is idempotent on
157	  `(run_id, seq)` (`INSERT OR IGNORE`), and `run_episode` re-sends its complete in-memory event list at
158	  `run.finished`, which back-fills any gap. A redeploy briefly overlaps old and new Store containers; the
159	  same idempotent re-send covers the overlap for runs that finish, and the ≤15 s window is documented for
160	  runs that don't.
161	- **Ids.** `<prefix>_<hex ms timestamp><12 hex random>` (sortable, no extra dependency): `c_`, `r_`, `m_`,
162	  `b_`, `l_`. User ids are minted by the browser as `u_<uuid4>`.
163	- **Migration (done 2026-09-12, ~19:00 EDT).** `harness/store.py` `RunStore` is now a thin client over
164	  `Store` (`harness/sqlite_store.py`), so `loop.py` and `api.py` kept their verbs; the loop appends
165	  events per step. The Modal Dict `faultline-runs` is no longer used; its 21 runs were imported once with
166	  `modal_app.py::import_legacy_runs` (`runs/20260912T230110Z_store/`).
167	- **Ops.** `modal volume get faultline-db faultline.sqlite3 runs/` yields a consistent file for local
168	  `sqlite3` inspection and for the evidence folder. Retention: none for the take-home (a `DELETE`
169	  archives; nothing is hard-deleted).
170	- **Known limits (accepted).** One Store container; full-file snapshot per checkpoint. Comfortable to
171	  hundreds of MB; past that, ship incrementally (Litestream-style, or `modal-mosql`) or move to Postgres.
172	  A single-writer actor is also a throughput ceiling (thousands of events/s — orders of magnitude above
173	  need).
174	
175	### 4.4 Schema (`services/agent-harness/harness/migrations/0001_init.sql`)
176	
177	The migration file is canonical; this section summarises it (a full copy of the DDL used to live here and
178	drifted). All timestamps are ISO-8601 UTC; JSON columns hold pydantic dumps from `faultline_common.schemas`.
179	
180	| Table | Key | Columns worth knowing |
181	|---|---|---|
182	| `users` | `id` (`u_<uuid4>`, minted by the browser, §6) | `created_at`, `last_seen_at`, `user_agent` |
183	| `conversations` | `id` (`c_…`) | `user_id`, `scenario_id`, `title` (defaults to the scenario title), `updated_at` (bumped on every event batch; drives list order), `archived_at` (soft delete) |
184	| `runs` | `id` (`r_…`) | `conversation_id`, `user_id` (denormalised for ownership checks), `scenario_id`, `model`, `seed`, `max_steps`, `task_prompt`, `episode_id`, `status` CHECK over all seven `RunStatus` values (`queued running ok error truncated unevaluated interrupted`), `score`, `evaluation_json`, token totals, `error`, timestamps, `steps`, `summary` (the agent's submit summary), `extra_json` (additive `RunRecord` fields) |
185	| `events` | `(run_id, seq)`, `WITHOUT ROWID` | append-only source of truth; `seq` == `Event.id` == SSE id; `type`, `step`, `data` |
186	| `messages` | `id` (`m_…`), unique `(conversation_id, seq)` | `run_id`, `role` user\|assistant, `step` (NULL for the task prompt) |
187	| `blocks` | `id` (`b_…`), unique `(message_id, seq)` | `type` text\|thinking\|tool_use\|tool_result, `text`, `tool_name`, `tool_use_id` (join key), `input_json`, `is_error`, `exit_code`, `duration_ms`, `fault_json`, `truncated` |
188	| `llm_calls` | `id` (`l_…`), unique `(run_id, step, attempt)` | `model`, `request_id`, `stop_reason`, four token counters, `started_at`, `duration_ms`, `error` |
189	
190	Relationships: `users 1─∞ conversations 1─∞ runs 1─∞ events`; `runs 1─∞ messages 1─∞ blocks`;
191	`runs 1─∞ llm_calls`. `events` is the source of truth; `messages`/`blocks`/`llm_calls` are projections
192	written in the same transaction (dropping them is scope cut #5 in the plan — the web reducer can rebuild
193	a transcript from events alone).
194	
195	### 4.5 Event → transcript projection
196	
197	Performed inside `Store.append_events`, per event, in the transaction that inserts the event row:
198	
199	| Event | Effect |
200	|---|---|
201	| `run.started` | `runs.status='running'`, `started_at`; `conversations.updated_at` |
202	| `episode.reset` | `runs.episode_id`; user message (`step NULL`) with one `text` block = `task_prompt` |
203	| `turn.thinking`, `turn.text`, `tool.call` (same `step`) | one assistant message for that step (created on first block); blocks appended in event order: `thinking`, `text`, `tool_use{tool_name, tool_use_id, input_json}` |
204	| `tool.result` (same `step`) | one user message for that step (Anthropic requires tool results in a user turn); `tool_result{tool_use_id, text=output, is_error, exit_code, duration_ms, fault_json}` |
205	| `llm.call` | `llm_calls` row; running totals into `runs.input_tokens/output_tokens` |
206	| `episode.evaluated` | `runs.score`, `runs.evaluation_json` (no message is projected; the submit summary is stored in `runs.summary`) |
207	| `run.finished` | `runs.status`, `finished_at`, `error`, final usage; `conversations.updated_at`; marks the DB dirty for checkpoint |
208	| `fault.fired`, `workspace.diff`, `log` | event row only; the UI reads them from the event log |
209	
210	Rebuilding the model's view of step *n*: take the conversation's messages for the run with `seq` up to
211	the assistant message of step *n − 1* plus its tool-result message, expand blocks in `seq` order, map
212	`text/thinking/tool_use/tool_result` one-to-one onto Anthropic content blocks. The UI instead groups each
213	`tool_result` under its `tool_use` by `tool_use_id` and renders it as a tool chip inside the assistant turn.
214	
215	## 5. HTTP contracts
216	
217	### 5.1 Harness API (`faultline-harness/api`, CORS `*`, all routes below `/scenarios` and `/health` require `X-Faultline-User`)
218	
219	| Route | Body → Response |
220	|---|---|
221	| `GET /health` | `Health{svc:"harness", ok, model_default, sandbox_env_url, has_provider_key:false, detail:{store:{ok,last_checkpoint_at}}}` |
222	| `GET /scenarios` | proxied from sandbox-env (public fields only) |
223	| `GET /me` | upsert → `{user_id, conversations: n}` |
224	| `GET /conversations` | `[{id, title, scenario_id, created_at, updated_at, last_run:{id,status,score}|null}]` newest first, archived excluded |
225	| `POST /conversations` | `{scenario_id, title?}` → `Conversation` |
226	| `GET /conversations/{id}` | `{conversation, runs:[RunSummary], messages:[Message{blocks:[Block]}]}` |
227	| `PATCH /conversations/{id}` | `{title?, archived?}` → `Conversation` |
228	| `DELETE /conversations/{id}` | soft archive → `{archived:true}` |
229	| `POST /conversations/{id}/runs` | `{model?, seed?, max_steps?, task_prompt?}` → `{run_id}` (202) |
230	| `POST /runs` | `{scenario_id, model?, seed?, max_steps?}` → creates a conversation then a run → `{run_id, conversation_id}` |
231	| `GET /runs/{id}` | `RunRecord` (record + all events + evaluation + usage) |
232	| `GET /runs/{id}/events` | SSE; `id:` = `seq`; honours `Last-Event-ID`; server closes at ~110 s |
233	
234	Ownership: every `/conversations/*` and `/runs/*` route resolves the resource, compares `user_id` with
235	the header, and returns 404 on mismatch (not 403 — no existence oracle).
236	
237	Wire shapes (JSON; the pydantic models in `faultline_common.schemas` and `apps/web/src/lib/types.ts`
238	mirror these field for field; DB columns `input_json` / `fault_json` / `evaluation_json` are parsed
239	into `input` / `fault` / `evaluation` on the wire):
240	
241	```ts
242	type User                = { id: string; created_at: string; last_seen_at: string }
243	type Conversation        = { id: string; user_id: string; scenario_id: string; title: string;
244	                             created_at: string; updated_at: string; archived_at: string | null }
245	type ConversationSummary = { id: string; title: string; scenario_id: string; created_at: string; updated_at: string;
246	                             last_run: { run_id: string; status: RunStatus; score: number | null } | null }
247	type Block               = { id: string; seq: number; type: 'text' | 'thinking' | 'tool_use' | 'tool_result';
248	                             text?: string | null; tool_name?: string | null; tool_use_id?: string | null;
249	                             input?: Record<string, unknown> | null; is_error?: boolean | null; exit_code?: number | null;
250	                             duration_ms?: number | null; fault?: FaultFired | null; truncated: boolean }
251	type Message             = { id: string; conversation_id: string; run_id: string; seq: number;
252	                             role: 'user' | 'assistant'; step?: number | null; created_at: string; blocks: Block[] }
253	type LlmCall             = { id: string; run_id: string; step: number; attempt: number; model: string;
254	                             request_id?: string | null; stop_reason?: string | null;
255	                             input_tokens?: number | null; output_tokens?: number | null;
256	                             cache_read_tokens?: number | null; cache_write_tokens?: number | null;
257	                             started_at: string; duration_ms?: number | null; error?: string | null }
258	type RunSummary          = { id: string; run_id: string /* same value, kept for the CLI */; status: RunStatus;
259	                             scenario_id: string; model: string; score: number | null; created_at: string;
260	                             finished_at: string | null; events?: number }
261	// RunRecord gains three optional fields: conversation_id?, user_id?, task_prompt?
262	// Event.data for the two new types:
263	//   'llm.call'      { attempt, model, stop_reason, usage: { input_tokens, output_tokens,
264	//                     cache_read_input_tokens?, cache_creation_input_tokens? }, request_id?, duration_ms }
265	//   'turn.thinking' { text }
266	// Responses:
267	//   GET  /me                        → { user_id: string; conversations: number }
268	//   GET  /conversations             → ConversationSummary[]
269	//   POST /conversations             → Conversation
270	//   GET  /conversations/{id}        → { conversation: Conversation; runs: RunSummary[]; messages: Message[] }
271	//   PATCH /conversations/{id}       → Conversation          DELETE → { archived: true }
272	//   POST /conversations/{id}/runs   → { run_id: string; conversation_id: string }
273	//   POST /runs                      → { run_id: string; conversation_id: string }
274	```
275	
276	As implemented, a request to `GET /runs` without `X-Faultline-User` is not scoped to a user (the CLI and
277	the contract checkers rely on it), which contradicts "never listed" in §6 — open item with the Store
278	owner.
279	
280	### 5.2 Sandbox-env (gym + MCP)
281	
282	As `PLAN.md` §2.2: REST `POST /episodes` (reset), `GET /episodes/{id}` (observe),
283	`POST /episodes/{id}/evaluate`, `DELETE /episodes/{id}`, `GET /scenarios`, `GET /health`, plus the ops
284	routes `GET /episodes` and `POST /episodes/sweep` (`docs/sandbox-env-ops.md`); MCP tools
285	`run_command`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `list_dir` at `/mcp`, episode selected by the
286	`X-Faultline-Episode` header so the model can never name another episode.
287	
288	### 5.3 Event stream
289	
290	Event types and payloads are defined in `faultline_common.schemas.Event` (`run.started`, `episode.reset`,
291	`turn.thinking`, `turn.text`, `tool.call`, `tool.result`, `llm.call`, `fault.fired`, `workspace.diff`,
292	`episode.evaluated`, `run.finished`, `log`; §5.4 adds `interruption`, `run.resumed` and `episode.sandbox`
293	to the contract, not yet emitted). `Event.id` is the 0-based sequence within the run and doubles
294	as the SSE id, the `events.seq` primary key, and the resume cursor. Events are produced by exactly one
295	writer per run (`run_episode`) and are never edited.
296	
297	### 5.4 Reliability event contract (confirmed with the harness lead 2026-09-12 ~22:45 UTC)
298	
299	**Status (2026-09-12 19:40 EDT): contract only.** Every field below is defined in `schemas.py` and the
300	docs, but no service emits it yet: sandbox-env still answers a lost sandbox with `EINTERNAL` and has no
301	`/interruptions` route, and the harness has no crash/resume path. The lead is implementing it.
302	
303	Canonical: `packages/common/faultline_common/schemas.py` (the `Event` docstring lists every data
304	shape), labels in `docs/error-taxonomy.md`, injector behaviour in `services/sandbox-env/FAULTS.md`.
305	The UI is a ten-second reliability read — *task → fault (simulated or real, which layer) → did the
306	command execute (executed / failed / not executed / unknown are four different answers) → recovery
307	(agent actions vs harness retries; did the sandbox survive) → diff + independent checks* — and it
308	**never derives an outcome from error text**: anything absent renders as "unknown" with the raw
309	payload expandable. Fields (all additive):
310	
311	| Where | Field | Meaning |
312	|---|---|---|
313	| `tool.result.data` | `outcome: executed \| failed \| not_executed \| unknown` | executed = ran and succeeded; failed = ran and reported failure; not_executed = refused/short-circuited before the sandbox saw it; unknown = may or may not have run (ack lost, transport, worker interrupted) |
314	| `tool.result.data` | `error_class` (iff `is_error`): `{origin: injected\|staged\|real, layer: boundary\|filesystem\|sandbox\|transport\|harness\|model\|gym, code, kind?, label, outcome_known, side_effect_applied?, detail?}` | provenance and layer; `label` is a fixed string rendered verbatim; `error_code` kept for compatibility; raw text stays in `output` |
315	| `tool.result.data` | `attempts`, `sandbox: {id, alive}` | harness-level retries of this call (1 = none); identity/liveness of the workspace that answered |
316	| `fault.fired.data` | `origin` (injected \| staged, never real), `layer` (boundary for injected, filesystem for staged), `description` | one plain sentence per fault |
317	| `episode.reset.data` | `sandbox_id` (short), `attempt` | workspace identity; >1 when re-provisioned |
318	| `episode.sandbox` (new) | `{sandbox_id, status: alive\|terminated\|replaced, reason, step}` | the environment noticed the worker changed |
319	| `interruption` (new) | `Interruption {step?, layer, code, label, tool_use_id?, tool?, path?, outcome_known, planned, resumed, worker_generation, at, detail?}` | always real; `planned: true` when a scenario `harness_faults` entry triggered it |
320	| `run.resumed` (new) | `{worker_generation, resumed_from_event_id, dangling_tool_use_id?, resumed_at}` | a fresh worker picked the run up |
321	| `run.finished.data` | `evaluation_status: ok\|failed\|skipped`, `evaluation_error?`, `steps`, `score?` | a run that could not be graded is `unevaluated`, never "ok" |
322	| `RunStatus` | `+ unevaluated`, `+ interrupted` | "ok" now implies a score |
323	| `RunRecord` | `error_class?`, `interruptions[]`, `worker_generation` | why the run ended as it did |
324	| `GET /scenarios` | `checks: [{id, description, weight}]`, `faults_public: [{kind, origin, layer, description}]`, `harness_faults: [{kind: worker_crash\|transport_abort, tool, path, nth, after_ms}]` | intended outcome and fault classes known before the run; the agent never sees them |
325	| codes (final) | `ENOENT EACCES ETIMEDOUT EINVAL ESANDBOX ETRANSPORT EHARNESS EMODEL EGYM EINTERNAL ENOEPISODE` | `ETIMEDOUT` is ack-lost *or* a real client timeout — origin/layer tell them apart |
326	
327	Older records (e.g. `r_e9bc5c8c6739`) predate these fields; the UI resolves what it can from the
328	evaluate ledger (`outcome: ack_lost` ⇒ the write landed, `short_circuit` ⇒ nothing ran) and says
329	"unknown" otherwise.

330	
331	## 6. Identity
332	
333	- **Minting.** `src/lib/identity.ts`: read `localStorage["faultline.user_id"]`, else the `faultline_uid`
334	  cookie, else mint `"u_" + crypto.randomUUID()`; write back to both. Cookie attributes:
335	  `SameSite=Lax; Secure; Max-Age=31536000; Path=/`. Not `HttpOnly` (JS writes it). All storage access is
336	  wrapped in try/catch; a browser that blocks storage still works for the session with an in-memory id.
337	- **Transport.** The harness API lives on a different origin from the SPA, so cookies are never sent to
338	  it. The id travels as `X-Faultline-User` on every request; the harness CORS config allows that header.
339	- **Why both stores.** localStorage is the primary (no size/attribute quirks, not sent anywhere);
340	  the cookie only exists so the id survives a "clear site data → local storage" that leaves cookies.
341	- **Threat model.** This is scoping, not authentication (explicit non-goal). Ids are 122-bit random and
342	  never listed, so they are unguessable in practice, but anyone holding one can read that browser's
343	  transcripts. Nothing sensitive is stored: scenarios are bundled, the workspace is a fixture, and the
344	  provider key never leaves `run_episode`. There is no per-id rate limit on `POST …/runs` yet.
345	
346	## 7. Web application
347	
348	### 7.1 Stack
349	
350	Vite 8 + React 19 + TypeScript 6, Tailwind v4 (`@tailwindcss/vite`), shadcn v4 CLI (style `base-nova`
351	on Base UI, `neutral` base colour, CSS variables, the `cn` helper package, lucide icons) for primitives,
352	Beautiful UI for the AI-native composites, vitest (jsdom). Path routes with no router dependency
353	(`src/lib/router.ts`, §7.3). **Node ≥ 22.12 is required** (Vite 8
354	`^20.19 || >=22.12`, vitest 5 `^22.12 || ^24`; `.nvmrc` pins 22 and the Modal image installs Node 22).
355	Built into `dist/` (in the Modal image, or prebuilt via `FAULTLINE_WEB_PREBUILT`) and served by a Modal
356	Server running `serve.py`: SPA fallback for extension-less paths, `no-store` on `index.html` and
357	`config.json`, immutable `/assets/`, unified JSON request logs. `serve.py` writes `/config.json` from
358	`$HARNESS_URL` at container start; the SPA resolves the harness URL as injected
359	`window.__FAULTLINE_CONFIG__` → `/config.json` → `VITE_HARNESS_URL` → default, so the URL can change

467	## 9. Decision log
468	
469	| # | Decision | Alternatives considered | Why |
470	|---|---|---|---|
471	| 1 | Three Modal apps with a strict secret boundary | one app | harness swappable, environment gradeable without trusting the agent, static UI; the provider key exists in exactly one function |
472	| 2 | Faults injected at the tool boundary, not inside the shell | `chmod`, deleting files live, network faults | deterministic, seedable, invisible to the agent, gradeable from a ledger; ack-lost is only expressible at the boundary |
473	| 3 | `spawn` + event log + reconnecting SSE | run the loop inside the request | Modal web requests cap at 150 s |
474	| 4 | Persistence = SQLite on a Modal Volume | Modal Dict only; hosted Postgres; Modal Dict as hot path + SQLite archive | user asked for it; zero external services; a single file is trivial to export as evidence |
475	| 5 | Single-writer `Store` actor (`max_containers=1`) | every function opens the DB on the Volume; per-run DB files | Volumes have no distributed locking and last-write-wins; one process owning the file removes every consistency question |
476	| 6 | Hot DB on local disk, `VACUUM INTO` snapshots renamed atomically onto the Volume | run SQLite directly on the FUSE mount | WAL/locking on FUSE is undocumented, and background commits are not multi-file snapshots; Modal's own example copies-then-commits |
477	| 7 | Volume v1 | v2 | v2 needs an explicit flag and is marked experimental in the 1.5.5 CLI; no benefit for one file |
478	| 8 | `events` as source of truth + `messages`/`blocks`/`llm_calls` projections | events only; projections only | replay/SSE need the log; the UI and "what did the model see" need the message shape; projections are a cheap same-transaction write and a listed scope cut |
479	| 9 | Identity via `X-Faultline-User` header, minted in the browser, mirrored to a cookie | cookie-only; server-minted id | the API is cross-origin so cookies never reach it; localStorage is the reliable store; the cookie is a backup |
480	| 10 | Beautiful UI by copy-paste with lucide/shadcn substitutions | Vercel AI Elements (a real registry); shadcn only | matches the requested look; MIT; the private deps are small and swappable; scope cut #4 if it drags |
481	| 11 | Haiku 4.5 default, Sonnet/Opus 5 allowlisted | Sonnet default | cost and latency for a demo; the ack-lost scenario is more interesting when a cheaper model sometimes fails it |
482	| 12 | Path routes through a small in-house router (`src/lib/router.ts`: `pushState` + `useSyncExternalStore`) | `react-router-dom`; query params only (the scaffold's first design) | four screens with deep links that survive a refresh (`serve.py` SPA fallback); no dependency in a static SPA |
483	| 13 | Node 22 pinned (`.nvmrc`, `engines`, Modal image) | stay on the machine default 21.6 | Vite 8 and vitest 5 refuse to start on 21.6 (`styleText` missing from `node:util`) |
484	
485	## 10. Open questions
486	
487	- Should the composer allow editing the task prompt, or only choosing a scenario? Current answer: editable,
488	  stored in `runs.task_prompt`, because it makes the "conversation" framing honest. Revisit if it invites
489	  prompt-injection-shaped demos that distract from the reliability story.
490	- Do we need per-user rate limiting beyond `POST …/runs`? Probably not for review traffic.
491	- Checkpoint cadence (15 s) vs. cost of `VACUUM INTO` as the DB grows — measure once real runs exist and
492	  record the number in `PLAN.md` §0.

Tool reply2026-09-13T00:42:09.881Z
151	### 2.3 Harness loop (agent-harness/run_episode)
152	
153	- SDK: `anthropic` (Python). Client: `anthropic.Anthropic()` (reads `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` from the secret).
154	- Model: `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` env (default `claude-haiku-4-5`), per-run override from an allowlist
155	  (`claude-haiku-4-5`, `claude-sonnet-5`, `claude-opus-5`). Thinking: adaptive on models that support it; Haiku 4.5 = off.
156	- Tools: discovered from MCP `list_tools` and converted to Anthropic tool defs (+ a local `submit` tool
157	  that ends the episode with a summary). Parallel tool calls executed in order, all results returned in one user message.
158	- Loop: manual `while stop_reason == "tool_use"` (no beta dependency), `max_steps`, per-call timeouts,
159	  typed error chain (`RateLimitError` → retry w/ backoff; `APIStatusError ≥500` → retry; else fail the run cleanly).
160	- System prompt strategy (recovery-oriented): one command per step, verify state after any error,
161	  after a timeout **read before re-writing**, prefer idempotent edits, run tests before submitting.
162	- Events appended to the Store (`Store().append_events.remote(run_id, [events])`, batched per step,
163	  idempotent on `(run_id, seq)`) as they happen. The loop also keeps them in memory and re-sends the
164	  full list at `run.finished`, so a Store restart self-heals (§2.9):
165	
166	```
167	run.started {run_id, scenario, model, seed}
168	episode.reset {episode_id, files}
169	turn.text {step, text}                       # assistant prose
170	tool.call {step, tool, input, tool_use_id}
171	tool.result {step, tool_use_id, output, is_error, duration_ms, fault?}   # fault echoed by sandbox-env public ledger
172	llm.call {step, attempt, model, stop_reason, usage:{input_tokens, output_tokens, cache_read_input_tokens, cache_creation_input_tokens}, request_id, duration_ms}   # one per messages.create — the "LLM turn"
173	turn.thinking {step, text}                   # thinking block (summary) when adaptive thinking is on
174	fault.fired {step, kind, path, mode}         # emitted by harness after reading ledger delta
175	workspace.diff {step, files, diffs}          # from observe, after each mutating step
176	episode.evaluated {score, checks, tests}
177	run.finished {status: ok|error|truncated, usage:{input_tokens, output_tokens}, duration_ms}
178	log {level, msg, ...}                        # verbose unified log lines mirrored into the stream
179	```
180	
181	Why not run the loop inside the SSE request: Modal web endpoints cap requests at **150 s**
182	(303-redirect polling after that breaks CORS). So `POST /runs` → `run_episode.spawn(...)` and the
183	browser tails `GET /runs/{id}/events` via SSE (the `api` polls `Store.events_after(run_id, after_seq)`
184	every 400 ms); the server closes each stream at ~110 s and the browser reconnects with
185	`Last-Event-ID` (= `events.seq`). `GET /runs/{id}` returns the whole record (also what we save as
186	evidence).

262	### 2.9 Persistence: SQLite on a Modal Volume (harness `Store`)
263	
264	Full write-up, DDL and decision log: `ARCHITECTURE.md` §4. This section is the plan-level summary.
265	
266	**What is persisted.** The browser's anonymous identity, its conversations, every run, the append-only
267	event log of each run, and a transcript projection (messages → content blocks, plus one `llm_calls`
268	row per `messages.create`). Episode state (fault plan, ledger) stays in sandbox-env's
269	`modal.Dict "faultline-episodes"` — ephemeral by design and never readable by the harness.
270	
271	**Implemented** (swarm Extend; live since ~19:00 EDT, evidence `runs/20260912T230110Z_store/`):
272	`harness/sqlite_store.py` (`SqliteStore`) behind the `Store` Modal class in `modal_app.py`; `harness/store.py`
273	`RunStore` is a thin client over it, so `loop.py`/`api.py` kept their verbs and the loop appends events per
274	step. The Modal Dict `faultline-runs` is no longer used; its 21 runs were imported once
275	(`modal_app.py::import_legacy_runs`).
276	
277	**Modal facts that shape the design** (docs + `modal==1.5.5`, verified 2026-09-12):
278	- Volumes are "write-once, read-many"; writes become visible to other containers only after a commit
279	  (background commits every few seconds, plus explicit `.commit()`); other readers must `.reload()`.
280	- "Last write wins in case of concurrent modification of the same file"; no distributed file locking.
281	- Modal's own SQLite example (Datasette) builds the DB on local disk, copies it onto the Volume, commits.
282	- Volume v2 needs explicit `version=2`; the 1.5.5 CLI marks it experimental → v1.
283	
284	**Decision — one writer, hot copy on local disk, consistent snapshots on the Volume.**
285	- `Store` = Modal class, `max_containers=1` (single writer; the only process that ever opens the DB),
286	  `min_containers=1` (no cold start per SSE poll). `@modal.concurrent(max_inputs=32)` + a
287	  `threading.Lock` around the connection; ops are sub-millisecond on local disk.
288	- DB at `/tmp/faultline.sqlite3` (`journal_mode=WAL`, `synchronous=NORMAL`, `foreign_keys=ON`) —
289	  plain SQLite semantics, no FUSE locking/mmap questions.
290	- Checkpoint = `VACUUM INTO '/data/faultline.sqlite3.tmp'` → `os.replace` → `vol.commit()`. Always a
291	  complete, consistent single file (a background commit can never capture a half-written DB).
292	  Triggered after every `run.finished`, every 15 s while dirty, and in `@modal.exit`.
293	  `@modal.enter` restores `/data/faultline.sqlite3 → /tmp` (or creates an empty DB) and runs
294	  migrations (`PRAGMA user_version`, `harness/migrations/000N_*.sql`).
295	- Loss window: ≤ the last 15 s of an *in-flight* run if the Store container dies. Completed runs
296	  self-heal: `append_events` is idempotent on `(run_id, seq)` and `run_episode` re-sends its full
297	  in-memory event list at `run.finished`.
298	- Nobody else touches the file. `api` and `run_episode` call `Store().<method>.remote(...)`; reads need
299	  no `reload()` because the reader *is* the writer.
300	- Accepted limit: one Store container, full-file snapshot per checkpoint. Past ~100 MB move to
301	  incremental shipping (Litestream-style / `modal-mosql`) or Postgres. Not a take-home problem.
302	
303	#### 2.9.1 Identity (browser → `X-Faultline-User`)
304	- First visit: `id = "u_" + crypto.randomUUID()` → `localStorage["faultline.user_id"]` + cookie mirror
305	  `faultline_uid` (`SameSite=Lax; Secure; Max-Age=31536000`). Read order: localStorage → cookie → mint.
306	  Both live on the web origin; the cookie only exists to survive a localStorage wipe.
307	- The harness API is a different origin (`…-harness-api.modal.run`), so cookies never reach it
308	  (`api.ts` already sends `credentials: 'omit'`). Identity travels as the `X-Faultline-User` header on
309	  every request (CORS already allows `*` headers).
310	- Server: validate `^u_[0-9a-f-]{36}$`, upsert `users` (`last_seen_at`, `user_agent`), scope every
311	  conversation/run route to it; mismatch → 404. Scoping, not auth (§1 non-goal).
312	
313	#### 2.9.2 Data model (tables; DDL in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.4)
314	```
315	users 1─∞ conversations 1─∞ runs 1─∞ events            (append-only; source of truth; SSE id == seq)
316	                              runs 1─∞ messages 1─∞ blocks  (projection: Anthropic message/content-block shape)
317	                              runs 1─∞ llm_calls           (projection of llm.call: usage, stop_reason, request_id, attempt)
318	```
319	| Table | Key columns |
320	|---|---|
321	| `users` | `id` (u_…), `created_at`, `last_seen_at`, `user_agent` |
322	| `conversations` | `id` (c_…), `user_id`, `scenario_id`, `title`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `archived_at` |
323	| `runs` | `id` (r_…), `conversation_id`, `user_id`, `scenario_id`, `model`, `seed`, `max_steps`, `task_prompt`, `episode_id`, `status`, `score`, `evaluation_json`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `error`, `created_at`, `started_at`, `finished_at` |
324	| `events` | PK `(run_id, seq)`, `ts`, `type`, `step`, `data` JSON |
325	| `messages` | `id` (m_…), `conversation_id`, `run_id`, `seq` (unique per conversation), `role` user\|assistant, `step`, `created_at` |
326	| `blocks` | `id` (b_…), `message_id`, `seq`, `type` text\|thinking\|tool_use\|tool_result, `text`, `tool_name`, `tool_use_id`, `input_json`, `is_error`, `exit_code`, `duration_ms`, `fault_json`, `truncated` |
327	| `llm_calls` | `id` (l_…), `run_id`, `step`, `attempt`, `model`, `request_id`, `stop_reason`, token counts ×4, `started_at`, `duration_ms`, `error` |
328	
329	#### 2.9.3 Event → transcript projection (inside `append_events`, same transaction)
330	| Event | Projection |
331	|---|---|
332	| `episode.reset` | user message (run's first) with one `text` block = `task_prompt` |
333	| `turn.thinking` / `turn.text` / `tool.call` (same `step`) | one assistant message per step; blocks in event order: `thinking`, `text`, `tool_use{tool_name, tool_use_id, input_json}` |
334	| `tool.result` (same `step`) | one user message per step (Anthropic wire shape) with `tool_result{tool_use_id, text=output, is_error, exit_code, duration_ms, fault_json}` |
335	| `llm.call` | `llm_calls` row |
336	| `episode.evaluated` | `runs.score`, `runs.evaluation_json` (no message is projected; the submit summary is stored in `runs.summary`) |
337	| `run.started` / `run.finished` | `runs.status / started_at / finished_at / tokens / error`; `conversations.updated_at` |
338	| `fault.fired`, `workspace.diff`, `log` | events only (the UI reads them from the event log) |
339	
340	The UI groups each `tool_result` under its `tool_use` by `tool_use_id`. The same rows rebuild the exact
341	`messages=[…]` array the model saw — that is what "LLM turns during tool calls" means here.
342	
343	#### 2.9.4 Harness API additions (all scoped by `X-Faultline-User`)
344	| Route | Purpose |
345	|---|---|
346	| `GET /me` | upsert user → `{user_id, conversations: n}` |
347	| `GET /conversations` | `[{id, title, scenario_id, updated_at, last_run:{id,status,score}}]`, newest first, archived excluded |
348	| `POST /conversations` `{scenario_id, title?}` | create; title defaults to the scenario title |
349	| `GET /conversations/{id}` | conversation + runs + messages + blocks (the transcript) |
350	| `PATCH /conversations/{id}` `{title?, archived?}` · `DELETE` | rename / archive (soft) |
351	| `POST /conversations/{id}/runs` `{model?, seed?, max_steps?, task_prompt?}` | create run (`queued`) + `run_episode.spawn` → `{run_id}` |
352	| `POST /runs` | kept: creates a conversation then a run → `{run_id, conversation_id}` (used by `run_episode_cli.py`; the web app uses the conversation routes) |
353	| `GET /runs` · `GET /runs/{id}` · `GET /runs/{id}/events` (SSE) | unchanged shapes; backed by Store; `GET /runs` scoped to the user |
354

422	### 2.11 Failure provenance and real interruptions (added 2026-09-12 18:35 EDT)
423	
424	**Status (review 2026-09-12 19:25 EDT): contract only.** The types, docs and scenario JSON below exist; no
425	service emits these fields yet, the gym has no `/interruptions` route, and the harness has no crash/resume
426	path. Implementation is with the lead (harness + sandbox-env).
427	
428	The agent keeps seeing OS/HTTP-style errors; everything else gets provenance. Canonical types in
429	`schemas.py` (`ErrorClass`, `ToolOutcome`, `Interruption`, `HarnessFault`, `InterruptionReport`,
430	run statuses `unevaluated` / `interrupted`), contract for the UI in `docs/error-taxonomy.md`,
431	truthful per-injector semantics in `services/sandbox-env/FAULTS.md`.
432	
433	- **origin**: `injected` (short-circuited or ack-withheld at the boundary; nothing real failed) ·
434	  `staged` (the scenario really deleted the file at reset; the ENOENT is a real OS error) ·
435	  `real` (unplanned failure of sandbox / transport / harness / model / gym / filesystem).
436	- **outcome** per tool result: `executed | failed | not_executed | unknown`.
437	- **codes**: `ENOENT EACCES ETIMEDOUT EINVAL` (agent-facing, same text either way) +
438	  `ESANDBOX ETRANSPORT EHARNESS EMODEL EGYM EINTERNAL ENOEPISODE` (real only).
439	- **scenario `worker-crash`**: the harness worker really dies (`os._exit`) while its first
440	  changelog write is in flight; Modal retries `run_episode`; the new worker resumes from the
441	  persisted events, tells the gym (`POST /episodes/{id}/interruptions`) and hands the agent an
442	  `EHARNESS` result with outcome unknown. Grading reuses `verified_before_rewrite`. Its `fault_plan` is
443	  empty, so until the harness crash exists that check reports "fault never triggered".
444	- Real specimen that motivated this: run `r_ccda8780cbee` (sandbox killed by a concurrent `reap`)
445	  ended `ok` with `score: null` labelled `EINTERNAL` → must become `interrupted` / `ESANDBOX`.
446	- Checklist: [ ] sandbox-env provenance + `ESANDBOX` + staged `faults_fired` + interruptions route +
447	  scenario passthrough + safe `reap` · [ ] harness classification + statuses + resume + `worker_crash`
448	  / `transport_abort` · [ ] live proofs (`scripts/prove_interruptions.py` — not written yet; worker-crash + lost-ack +
449	  real sandbox loss) · [ ] verify skill: `features/interruptions.md` + stage · [ ] web labels (other session).
450

Tool reply2026-09-13T00:42:40.880Z
1	# Error and status taxonomy (contract for the UI, the ledger and the evidence)
2	
3	> **Status (2026-09-12 19:45 EDT): contract, not yet implemented.** Defined in
4	> `packages/common/faultline_common/schemas.py`; neither the harness nor sandbox-env emits these fields yet
5	> (`PLAN.md` §2.11, lead implementing). Until they do, a UI must render them as absent/unknown, never infer them.
6	
7	Two questions must be answerable for every failure a run shows: **who caused it** (`origin`) and
8	**which layer really failed** (`layer`). The agent never gets these; the UI always does.
9	
10	## `ErrorClass` (on every `tool.result` with `is_error`, on `fault.fired`, on `RunRecord.error_class`)
11	
12	```json
13	{"origin": "injected|staged|real", "layer": "boundary|filesystem|sandbox|transport|harness|model|gym",
14	 "code": "ENOENT|EACCES|ETIMEDOUT|EINVAL|ESANDBOX|ETRANSPORT|EHARNESS|EMODEL|EGYM|EINTERNAL|ENOEPISODE",
15	 "kind": "missing_file|denied_write|ack_lost|null", "label": "<fixed string below>",
16	 "outcome_known": true|false, "side_effect_applied": true|false|null, "detail": "…"}
17	```
18	
19	| origin / layer | code(s) | label (render verbatim) | outcome_known | side effect |
20	|---|---|---|---|---|
21	| injected / boundary | ENOENT | `simulated: missing file (file still on disk)` | true | no |
22	| injected / boundary | EACCES | `simulated: write denied (nothing written)` | true | no |
23	| injected / boundary | ETIMEDOUT | `simulated: lost ack (write landed; response withheld)` | **false** | **yes** |
24	| staged / filesystem | ENOENT | `staged: file absent since reset` | true | n/a |
25	| real / filesystem | ENOENT, EACCES, EINVAL | `real: OS error in sandbox` | true | no |
26	| real / sandbox | ESANDBOX | `real: sandbox terminated or unavailable` | true | no |
27	| real / transport | ETRANSPORT, ETIMEDOUT | `real: transport failure harness<->sandbox-env (outcome unknown)` | **false** | unknown |
28	| real / harness | EHARNESS | `real: harness worker interrupted mid-call (outcome unknown)` | **false** | unknown (ledger has truth after the fact) |
29	| real / model | EMODEL | `real: model API error` | true | — |
30	| real / gym | EGYM | `real: gym control-plane failure` | true | — |
31	| real / boundary | EINTERNAL, ENOEPISODE | `real: internal error in sandbox-env` | true | no |
32	
33	UI guidance: badge colour by `origin` (injected = amber "simulated", staged = amber "staged", real
34	= red "real"); show `label`; when `outcome_known: false` add "verify before retrying"; when the
35	ledger later resolves `side_effect_applied`, show it ("the write had landed").
36	
37	## Events
38	
39	- `fault.fired` — `FaultFired` + `origin` (`injected` or `staged`). Never `real`.
40	- `interruption` — `Interruption` (always real): `{step, layer, code, label, tool_use_id?, tool?, path?, outcome_known, planned, resumed, worker_generation, at, detail}`. `planned: true` means a scenario `harness_faults` entry triggered it (a deliberate real failure).
41	- `run.resumed` — `{worker_generation, resumed_from_event_id, dangling_tool_use_id?, resumed_at}` emitted by the worker that picked the run up.
42	- `tool.result` — `data.error_class` present iff `is_error`; `data.fault` (FaultFired) present iff a fault fired for this call.
43	
44	## Run status
45	
46	| status | meaning | score |
47	|---|---|---|
48	| `ok` | the agent submitted or ended; evaluation succeeded | number |
49	| `truncated` | step budget exhausted; still evaluated | number |
50	| `unevaluated` | the loop finished but `evaluate` failed (`error_class.layer: gym` or `sandbox`) — **not** the agent's fault | null |
51	| `interrupted` | a real interruption ended the run and no worker resumed it | null |
52	| `error` | the harness could not run the episode (model auth, reset failed, bug) | null |
53	
54	`RunRecord.interruptions[]` lists every real interruption, `worker_generation` is 1 + the number of
55	resumes; a resumed run can still end `ok` (that is the point of the `worker-crash` scenario).
56	
57	## Scenario metadata
58	
59	`Scenario.fault_kinds` (injected kinds in play) and `Scenario.harness_faults[]` (planned **real**
60	interruptions: `{kind: worker_crash|transport_abort, tool, path, nth, after_ms}`) are public so the
61	UI can show "includes a real harness interruption" on the card. The agent is never told either.
62	
63	## Ledger (released at evaluate)
64	
65	`LedgerEntry.origin` (`injected|staged|real`, only when `outcome != ok`), `error_code`, and
66	`interrupted: true` when the harness reported that it never received the response (`POST
67	/episodes/{id}/interruptions`). `verified_before_rewrite` treats `ack_lost` rows and `interrupted`
68	rows identically.
69

1	# Harness Store is live — notes for the apps/web owner
2	
3	Written 2026-09-12 23:2x UTC by the agent-harness owner. Everything below is measured against the
4	deployed harness (`https://appliedlabsai-local--faultline-harness-api.modal.run`), not inferred.
5	Evidence: `runs/20260912T230110Z_store/` (store checks before/after a redeploy, three live runs, the
6	exported SQLite file).
7	
8	## TL;DR
9	
10	`/me`, `/conversations`, `/conversations/{id}`, `PATCH`/`DELETE /conversations/{id}` and
11	`POST /conversations/{id}/runs` **answer 200 now** — they used to 404, and your code's "history
12	unavailable" degradation path is no longer the one that fires. Nothing that already worked changed
13	shape: `scripts/web_check.py` is 22/22 and `services/agent-harness/tools/check_contract.py` is 23/23
14	against the new build.
15	
16	Two 500s you would have seen between ~23:01 and ~23:04 UTC (`GET /conversations` for
17	`u_00000000-0000-4000-8000-00000000beef`, "no such table: conversations") were mine: the first deploy
18	of the Store shipped without its migration file. Fixed and redeployed; the Store now refuses to start
19	rather than serving an empty database.
20	
21	## What persists
22	
23	SQLite on the Modal Volume `faultline-db`, written by a single `@app.cls(max_containers=1)` Store
24	container (PLAN.md §2.9, ARCHITECTURE.md §4). Survives a redeploy: proven by running the same
25	30-assertion check before and after `modal deploy` (`store_check_before.json`,
26	`store_check_after_redeploy.json`), and by pulling the file off the Volume and opening it with a
27	fresh `sqlite3` connection (`db/inspect.txt`: `integrity_check ok`, `user_version 1`, 600 events).
28	
29	## Routes (all shapes verified live)
30	
31	| Route | Response |
32	|---|---|
33	| `GET /me` | `{user_id, conversations: n}` — 400 if `X-Faultline-User` is missing or malformed |
34	| `GET /conversations` | **a bare array** of `ConversationSummary` (ARCHITECTURE §5.1), newest first, archived excluded. Your `normaliseList` already tolerates both shapes. |
35	| `POST /conversations` `{scenario_id, title?}` | `Conversation`, 200. Title defaults to the scenario's title. 400 for an unknown scenario. |
36	| `GET /conversations/{id}` | `{conversation, runs: RunSummary[], messages: Message[]}` — validated as `schemas.ConversationDetail` |
37	| `PATCH /conversations/{id}` `{title?, archived?}` | `Conversation` |
38	| `DELETE /conversations/{id}` | `{archived: true}` (soft; the conversation is still readable by id) |
39	| `POST /conversations/{id}/runs` `{model?, seed?, max_steps?, task_prompt?}` | **202** `{run_id, conversation_id, …}` |
40	| `POST /runs` | unchanged 200, and now also carries `conversation_id` |
41	| `GET /runs` | `{runs: [...]}`, rows carry `id` **and** `run_id`, plus `conversation_id` and `events` |
42	| `GET /runs/{id}`, `GET /runs/{id}/events` | unchanged (RunRecord; SSE with `retry: 1000`, 0-based ids, `event: done` with `reason: finished|window`, all three resume spellings) |
43	
44	## Identity and scoping (PLAN §2.9.1)
45	
46	* `X-Faultline-User: u_<uuid4>` is validated against `^u_<uuid4>$`, upserted, and used to scope.
47	* `/me` and every `/conversations*` route **require** it (400 otherwise).
48	* Ownership mismatch is **404, never 403** — no existence oracle. Verified for GET/PATCH/DELETE of a
49	  conversation, `POST /conversations/{id}/runs`, `GET /runs/{id}` and the SSE route.
50	* `GET /runs` is scoped to the caller when the header is present.
51	* **Runs created without the header** (the CLI, the smoke scripts) belong to one well-known anonymous
52	  user and stay readable by anybody — so old `?run=<id>` evidence links keep opening in the UI. Only
53	  runs owned by a real `u_…` are hidden from other browsers.
54	
55	## The persisted transcript (what `GET /conversations/{id}.messages` contains)
56	
57	Projection written in the same transaction as the event insert (ARCHITECTURE §4.5), so it is exactly
58	the `messages=[…]` array the model saw:
59	
60	* one `user` message with `step: null` — the task prompt, one `text` block;
61	* one `assistant` message **per step**: blocks in event order `thinking` → `text` → `tool_use`
62	  (`tool_name`, `tool_use_id`, `input` parsed, not a JSON string);
63	* one `user` message per step holding that step's `tool_result` blocks (`tool_use_id`, `text`,
64	  `is_error`, `exit_code` when the tool was `run_command`, `duration_ms`, `fault`, `truncated`).
65	
66	Group a result under its call by `tool_use_id`; every `tool_use` in a finished run has exactly one
67	`tool_result` (asserted live: 32/32 on the gauntlet transcript). `messages[].seq` is per-conversation
68	and monotonic, so a conversation with several runs renders as one thread.
69	
70	`fault.fired`, `workspace.diff` and `log` are **not** projected — read them from the event log as you
71	do today.
72	
73	## Token counters
74	
75	`llm.call.data.usage` now carries `cache_creation_input_tokens` as well as
76	`cache_read_input_tokens` (the gap flagged in `docs/harness-contract.md` is closed), and
77	`RunRecord.usage` carries all four counters. If you show a cost/token chip, note that
78	`input_tokens` is only the **uncached** tail once caching kicks in — e.g. the 27-step gauntlet run
79	reports `input_tokens: 8808` with `cache_read_input_tokens: 199090`. Summing `input_tokens` alone
80	now understates the context by an order of magnitude; render reads separately rather than adding
81	them in.
82	
83	Measured on `claude-haiku-4-5` (`runs/20260912T230110Z_store/run_*/events.jsonl`): cache writes start
84	at the step where the conversation prefix first exceeds 4096 tokens (the model's minimum cacheable
85	prefix), and reads start on the step after that — step 7 of 12 on `missing-config`, step 5 of 27 on
86	`gauntlet`. Before this change every call reported `cache_read_input_tokens: 0`, so a UI that only
87	lights up on a non-zero read will now light up mid-run, not at all.
88	
89	## Two things worth knowing
90	
91	1. `GET /conversations/{id}` returns the whole transcript in one response (55 messages / 329 blocks
92	   for the gauntlet run ≈ 200 KB). There is no pagination yet; if that becomes a problem, ask and I
93	   will add `?after_seq=`.
94	2. A conversation's `updated_at` is bumped on every batch of events, so the rail re-orders while a
95	   run is streaming. `last_run` in the summary is the newest run of that conversation, keyed by `id`
96	   **and** `run_id`.
97

1:# Faultline
15:## Try it in 60 seconds
25:## Architecture
50:## Simulated vs. real failures
77:## Repo layout
91:## Running it yourself
114:## Verifying it
130:## Logging
136:## Status and limits
50: ## Simulated vs. real failures
51: 
52: The harness now emits `error_class` with failure origin (`injected | staged | real`), layer and
53: human-readable label, plus command `outcome` (`executed | failed | not_executed | unknown`).
54: `unevaluated` identifies unavailable grading; `interrupted` identifies an interruption that ended
55: the run. The model receives the tool error, while the browser and evidence receive provenance.
56: See [the error taxonomy](docs/error-taxonomy.md) and [fault semantics](services/sandbox-env/FAULTS.md).
57: 
58: The **`worker-crash` scenario is implemented**: the harness process exits during a dispatched write,
59: Modal retries the same run, and a fresh worker reconstructs the conversation from persisted events
60: and reconnects to the existing episode. It supplies an unknown-outcome result for the pending call
61: so the agent can check the workspace before continuing. In the saved live proof, worker generation
62: advanced to 2, the ledger confirmed the write landed, and the final checks found exactly one release
63: entry and the correct version, scoring 100/100.
64: 
65: The saved interruption suite (`runs/20260913T001500Z_interruptions/summary.json`, gitignored local
66: evidence) passed 39/39 checks across worker crash, transport abort, sandbox loss, simulated lost-ack
67: and a prior failure specimen. The proof driver is [scripts/prove_interruptions.py](scripts/prove_interruptions.py).
68: These results are scoped to the tested runs:
69: 
70: - The crash proof uses `write_file`; a real shell-append crash experiment still needs separate verification.
71: - The trigger sleeps after dispatch, rather than waiting at a confirmed write-completion barrier.
72:   The ledger establishes that the write landed in the captured run, not that every crash occurs after it.
73: - Recovery assumes the environment and store survive. Forced persistence flushes still tolerate errors;
74:   simultaneous storage failure can lose recovery state. This is not a universal exactly-once guarantee.
75: - Four simulated-fault scenario replays are bundled; a worker-crash replay is not bundled yet.
76: 
77: ## Repo layout

    """Bound a string for log/event payloads, marking truncation explicitly."""
    if not isinstance(s, str) or len(s) <= limit:
        return s
    return s[:limit] + f"…[truncated {len(s) - limit} chars]"


def log_event(svc: str, ev: str, msg: str = "", lvl: str = "info", **extras: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Emit one JSON log line. Returns the record (handy for mirroring into event streams)."""
    if _LEVELS.get(lvl, 20) < _min_level():
        return {}
    rec: dict[str, Any] = {"ts": _now(), "svc": svc, "lvl": lvl}
    for key, var in (("run_id", ctx_run_id), ("episode_id", ctx_episode_id), ("step", ctx_step), ("request_id", ctx_request_id)):
        val = var.get()
        if val is not None:
            rec[key] = val
    rec["ev"] = ev
    rec["msg"] = msg
    for k, v in extras.items():
        if v is not None:
            rec[k] = v
    sys.stdout.write(json.dumps(rec, default=str, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
    sys.stdout.flush()
    return rec


class Logger:
    """Tiny bound logger: `log = get_logger("sandbox-env"); log.info("tool.result", "ok", tool="read_file")`."""

    def __init__(self, svc: str):
        self.svc = svc

    def debug(self, ev: str, msg: str = "", **kw: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return log_event(self.svc, ev, msg, "debug", **kw)

    def info(self, ev: str, msg: str = "", **kw: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return log_event(self.svc, ev, msg, "info", **kw)

    def warn(self, ev: str, msg: str = "", **kw: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return log_event(self.svc, ev, msg, "warn", **kw)

    def error(self, ev: str, msg: str = "", **kw: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return log_event(self.svc, ev, msg, "error", **kw)

    class _Timer:
        def __init__(self, outer: "Logger", ev: str, **kw: Any):
            self.outer, self.ev, self.kw = outer, ev, kw
            self.t0 = time.perf_counter()

        def __enter__(self) -> "Logger._Timer":
            return self

        def __exit__(self, *exc: Any) -> None:
            dur = int((time.perf_counter() - self.t0) * 1000)
            if exc[0] is not None:
                self.outer.error(self.ev, f"failed: {exc[1]}", dur_ms=dur, **self.kw)
            else:
                self.outer.info(self.ev, "ok", dur_ms=dur, **self.kw)

    def timed(self, ev: str, **kw: Any) -> "Logger._Timer":
        return Logger._Timer(self, ev, **kw)


def get_logger(svc: str) -> Logger:
    return Logger(svc)

Faultline second cleanup review: harness, common and scripts (read-only)

No files were changed. All 209 harness tests pass, but most of the earlier items are still in the code. The docs lag in the other direction: several still say the taxonomy and resume work is "contract only" or "not yet emitted", though live proofs now pass 65/65.

1. Status of previous items

# Status Evidence
B1 STILL PRESENT loop.py run_episode_sync reset block: scenario.get("task_prompt") or req.get("task_prompt"), then sink.update(task_prompt=…) overwrites the user's edit.
B2 FIXED The finally block sets unevaluated when evaluation_status != "ok". Covered by test_provenance::test_evaluate_failing_makes_the_run_unevaluated_not_ok.
B3 PARTLY FIXED classify_result now checks transport_kind before fault, so client timeouts are real/transport. But loop.inferred_fault/CODE_TO_KIND still attach fault{kind, inferred, origin: injected} whenever observe() fails. A real ENOENT/EACCES is then labelled "simulated". An unobserved ETIMEDOUT is still classified injected. mcp_client.transport_kind_of still maps any *Timeout* (including PoolTimeout) to ETIMEDOUT.
B4 STILL PRESENT (unplanned calls) tool.call is emitted, then dispatch() runs with no forced flush. Only the harness_plan trigger path calls flush(force=True). EVENT_FLUSH_S=0.25 coalesces the event.
B5 STILL PRESENT gym_client.RETRYABLE includes ReadTimeout, RemoteProtocolError and ReadError. _request applies it to reset() and evaluate() with retries=3.
B6 PARTLY FIXED Entrypoints now use _deployed_store(). Store.exit still calls checkpoint(force=True) unconditionally, and the ephemeral app still declares Store(min_containers=1).

3. Doc drift

In every item the code is right and the doc should change, unless noted.

  • schemas.py Event docstring is missing fields the code emits:
  • episode.reset: scenario, workspace_root (plan_resume reads both)
  • run.started: sandbox_env_url, worker_generation
  • tool.call: mutating
  • tool.result: summary, synthetic, reported_to_gym
  • run.finished: worker_generation, interruptions
  • run.resumed: step, dropped_thinking_blocks
  • episode.sandbox lists replaced, which is never sent.
  • Other schemas.py text:
  • RunStatus "interrupted (sandbox gone, worker died, ...)": in code only sandbox loss ends interrupted; a dead worker resumes.
  • HarnessFault docstring "the request is already at sandbox-env, so the side effect completes" rests on a sleep. The docstring should say so, or the code should add a barrier.
  • RunRequest lacks task_prompt and harness_faults, both of which _start_run accepts.
  • PROVENANCE.md:

4. Tests

Tests that lock in behaviour marked for removal or change: - test_loop::test_inferred_fault_only_for_known_codes (B3) - test_provenance::test_classification_table, case "...but with observe() down" → injected (B3) - test_provenance::test_the_fallback_only_applies_when_the_gym_published_nothing (§2 item 1) - test_api::test_runs_are_scoped_to_the_caller (last two asserts) and test_anonymous_runs_stay_readable_by_any_browser (B8) - test_events_prompts::test_index_… duplicates coverage already in test_store/test_api

Other observations: - No always-true (or True) tests in the harness suite. - test_worker_crash_kills_the_worker_only_after_the_request_is_dispatched depends on a real 10 ms thread sleep, so there is some flake risk.

Missing tests: - A mutating tool.call is persisted before dispatch at the production flush interval (B4). - Resume after submit (§2 item 5). - A run whose retries are exhausted (§2 item 9).
